ParlaMint-SI_2016-03-29-SDZ7-Redna-17.ana.u3	The matter was addressed by the Committee on Infrastructure, Environment and Space as a parent labour force. For the presentation of the report of the Committee, I give the floor to President Igor Zorčič.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-03-29-SDZ7-Redna-17.ana.u4	Mr President, thank you for saying so. Dear representatives of the Ministry, Madam Minister, distinguished Dr. Christmas, colleague and colleague! The Committee on Infrastructure, the Environment and Space is held at its 32nd emergency meeting, on 16. 3. 2016 as a parent working body considered the request of the Council of State that the National Assembly decide again on the Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Environmental Protection Act submitted by the Government to the Assembly, which was adopted at its 16th session. 3. This Regulation shall enter into force on the twentieth day following that of its publication in the Official Journal of the European Union. The State Council's decision-making request was presented by the representative of the Council of State, Dr. Zoran Bozič, who presented the opinion of the Council of State, in which he pointed out that the Council of State, as the key reasons for the request, cited disagreement in particular with the solutions provided for in Articles 7 and 10 of the Law. With regard to the provision of Article 7, which removes the time limit for the validity of an environmental authorisation and thus makes it mandatory to verify the conditions laid down for the operation of an installation every 10 years, the transparency of the whole procedure is reduced in the opinion of the State Council and prevents the exercise of the right to involve and co-decision the public in the extension of environmental permits. The procedure for granting or extending an environmental authorisation has so far been considered by the local population and the public concerned to be actively involved in the decision on the operation or placement of environmentally harmful activities in a particular local environment. The State Council notes that the current legislation allows for the extension of the environmental protection authorisation if the device meets those requirements which apply to the issue of an environmental protection authorisation for a new installation at the time of the decision. This is said to be no longer the case and, in their view, concerns about the proposed legal changes to environmental protection standards will deteriorate further. The State Council has also pointed out, as inadequate, the provisions of Article 10 which allow for lower emission limit values to be established under certain conditions in the environmental authorisation, as determined on the basis of the level of emissions of BAT conclusions, provided that higher limit values would result in disproportionately higher costs compared to environmental benefits. In the opinion of the Council of State, such arrangements are clearly in the interest of capital and not of the protection of the environment and of the people living in it. As a key problem, however, the State Council points out that the provision does not clearly define how and by what basis environmental pollution will be assessed or the level of disproportionately higher costs compared to environmental benefits is not clearly defined. On behalf of the Government, the Minister for Environment and Space, Mrs Irena Majcen, stressed that the Government rejects the opinion of the Council of State that the amendment of the law poses a greater risk for environmental burdens, as well as the view that human legislation does not already protect people sufficiently. It pointed out that the legislation in force in the field of environmental protection was fully in line with EU legislation, both for emission limits and environmental quality standards as well as for all other institutions and instruments of environmental protection contained in EU law. All facilities in our country must meet the conditions laid down by EU regulations on environmental protection, which are particularly strict with regard to large combustion plants and waste incineration. With regard to the elimination of the time limit on the validity of the environmental licence, it is explained that the amendment provides reasons for the proposed solutions of a substantive nature and are linked to both the solutions in force and the amended and supplemented solutions in the amendment of the law. These set out cases where an environmental authorisation has to be amended either for the purpose of the operator or the operator of the operation or on an official basis. With regard to the regulation in Article 10 of the Law, where, in certain cases and under certain conditions in an environmental authorisation, a less stringent limit value or measures as set out in the BAT conclusions can be established, it is clarified that such an arrangement is contained in Directive 2010/75/EU itself because of such arrangements cannot be considered as an arrangement which would in itself be of particular interest to the capital and, consequently, contrary to the public interest or protection of the environment and people. In the debate they expressed their support for the request of the Council of State in the Joint Left Group and representatives of NGOs ECO Circle and ECO Anhovo and reiterated their views on the second reading of the proposed amendment of the law on the Committee. With regard to the state Council's exposure to the suspension of the 10-year time limit on the validity of an environmental permit, it has been pointed out by the coalition Members that the applicable law in Article 77 provides for the obligation of the competent authority to review and amend the applicable environmental permit for a particular device whenever its operator intends to make an amendment to the device. As regards the position of non-governmental organisations on the transposition obligation, it was reiterated that a communication from the European Commission was also sent in this regard, which takes the view that such a provision should be adopted in the Slovenian legal order. ParlaMint-SI_2016-03-29-SDZ7-Redna-17.ana.u7	In order to explain my opinion, I give the floor to the representative of the Government as a bill-maker. Ms Irena Majcen, Minister for the Environment and Space, has the floor.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-03-29-SDZ7-Redna-17.ana.u8	Dear President of the National Assembly, Dr. Milan Brglez, honourable Members and Members of Parliament, distinguished guests! Allow me to briefly point out two key amendments to the draft Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Environmental Protection Act, which raised the most opposition during the examination of the law. With regard to the elimination of the time limit on the validity of the environmental licence, I would first of all like to point out that Directive 2010/75/EU does not contain a requirement for Member States to limit the validity of licences. The Republic of Slovenia has been among the rare countries, if not the only ones where such a time limit has been imposed. The Directive only requires the competent authority to verify at regular intervals and, where appropriate, update the conditions in the authorisation if the applicable law in Article 77 provides for the obligation of the competent authority to review and amend the applicable authorisation for a particular device whenever its operator intends to make an amendment to the device. The applicable law also lays down, together with the proposed amendments and additions to Article 78, the obligation of the competent authority to review and amend, in certain cases, the applicable environmental protection authorisation following an official duty. I would also like to point out that we are proposing to supplement the current regulation whereby the competent authority has to review and, if necessary, amend the environmental protection authorisation every 10 years if the environmental protection authorisation has not been amended at that time for any other reason. It should also be noted that the operation of all installations and installations under continuous inspection, in particular devices that may lead to pollution of the environment of a larger scale and installations, is under continuous inspection. The public and the public concerned also have the same rights after amending the current regime as regards participation in the authorisation and modification procedures as in the applicable regulation in accordance with the requirements of the relevant Directives. This means that it is not possible to talk about preventing the right of the public to participate and co-decision in the environmental authorisation process. With regard to the possibility of certain less stringent limit values, the Ministry will be able to set less stringent emission limit values than determined on the basis of the BAT conclusions emission level only if a set of conditions is met. Once again, I would like to point out that the regulation itself is difficult enough to prevent environmental destruction in Slovenia. Additional arrangements are also planned as an amendment to the IED Regulation, so that more detailed guidance is also provided for the preparation of the assessment of disproportionately higher costs and the environmental benefits. But it is true that the commission's instructions on this subject are not yet available. Otherwise, it is already laid down in the law so that it is not about automaticism. The operation of the device will not cause significant pollution of the environment, a high level of environmental protection as a whole will have to be maintained and the limit value must not exceed the emission limit values prescribed under Article 17 of the Law, which at the same time means the values set out in the various Directives. The Ministry of its decision shall rely on the results of the monitoring of the state of the environment, which shall be carried out continuously, in accordance with the rules. The operator will have to make a specific assessment showing that achieving emission levels related to the best available techniques, as described in the BAT conclusions, would result in disproportionately higher costs compared to environmental benefits due to geographical location or local environmental conditions of the device or its technical characteristics. The assessment will have to be made in accordance with BAT reference documents and other documents published by the European Commission in relation to the setting of emission limit values and technical measures. It will not be possible to set less stringent emission limit values if this jeopardises the achievement of environmental quality standards or if the device is located in an area declared by the Government to be degraded. However, it is an extremely important fact that less stringent limit values will be set in a process of issuing an environmental permit, in which, as party participants in accordance with the law, interested public representatives who can use all legal means are involved. During the public hearing, different interpretations emerged concerning the transposition of Article 15/4 of the Law on Environmental Protection and Treatment in the State Council and the National Assembly. When transferring the Directive, the ILO referred to the instructions on how to transpose the Directive, which was published on the Commission’s website. The document shall indicate that the transposition of this Article is compulsory. Information from a non-governmental organisation came to the public later in order to give the Commission the view that the transposition of this Article is not mandatory. In view of the various information and interpretations, the ILO asked the Commission for an explanation received on 23 March and by which the Commission confirms the obligation to transpose this Article. The Commission further states in the communication that the communication is not an official position of the Commission, since only the EU court has the power of official interpretation of the Board of Governors. At the same time, I would like to say that some who do not find it difficult to pollute space [[…]]

ParlaMint-SI_2016-03-29-SDZ7-Redna-17.ana.u18	Dear President, dear Dr. Christmas, representatives of the Ministry, colleague and colleague! The National Assembly shall meet at its 16th session, on 4. 3. 2016 adopted a new law on environmental protection, which largely notifys environmental directives of the European Union. The Council of State then adopted a request for a re-decision on this law on the grounds that certain legal provisions pose an increased risk of environmental burden. The opinion of the State Council mainly follows the comments of non-governmental organisations which object to the legal provisions adopted, in particular, to the work relating to the termination of the period of validity of the environmental permit and the derogation from the best available technologies, so-called BAT conclusions, in cases where this would constitute an incomparable higher cost for an investor without having a significant negative impact on the environment. With regard to the termination of the period of validity of the environmental authorisation, it should be pointed out that Article 21 of the IED Directive, which states that a Member State must take measures to verify the conditions of authorisation at regular intervals. Before the amendment of the law in question, it was considered that the operator of the activity should notify the Ministry of any proposed change, on the basis of which the environmental permit is also being updated accordingly. The Ministry of Environment and Space shall also carry out checks on environmental permits for a maximum period of 10 years. At the same time, any installation or establishment under continuous supervision shall be the competent inspection service. In doing so, it should be stressed once again that the amendment of legislation is without prejudice to or altering the role and position of the public in decision-making processes to amend the environmental permit. The second provision, which was identified as controversial by non-governmental organisations, is the possibility that the Government may set less stringent limits as provided for in the conclusions of the complaints. The setting of lower standards will only be possible in cases where this does not jeopardise the achievement of existing environmental quality standards, when significant environmental pollution is not caused and a high level of environmental protection as a whole is maintained. Different interpretations emerged as to whether part of the Directive relating to emission reductions is mandatory or optional part of the transposition into the Slovenian legal order. With regard to this dilemma, the Ministry of the Environment and Space has obtained the opinion of the European Commission, which clearly shows that the implementation of this content is binding on all EU Member States. Members of the Modern Centre Party had the opportunity to re-examine different opinions, to obtain additional information and explanations in order to assess as objectively as possible whether changes to the Environmental Protection Act really pose risks in the field of environmental protection. We have found that this is not the case, so we will re-support the Environmental Protection Act in the proposed form. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2016-03-29-SDZ7-Redna-17.ana.u38	Dear President, Secretary of State, Members of Parliament, a very nice greeting! The proposal for a law amending and supplementing the Law on the Market in Financial Instruments was based on the transposition of the three directives and the adaptation of Slovenian legislation by two regulations. As a result, this means addressing the shortcomings and inconsistencies of the current law on the market in financial instruments related to European legislation. The financial crisis in 2007, 2008 revealed major weaknesses in financial supervision, in individual cases as well as in relation to the financial system as a whole. During the crisis, there were shortcomings in cooperation, coordination, consistent application of Union law and trust between the competent authorities of the Member States. Unacceptable risks related to the regulation of financial institutions have also been identified. In order to address the regulatory shortcomings that emerged during the crisis, a large-scale reform was needed. Part of this is also the simplification of the legislative provisions of the Member States and the introduction of uniform prudential rules for institutions, which is essential for the functioning of the internal market. What does this implementation mean for the Republic of Slovenia with regard to participation in the European financial surveillance system? Firstly, the Securities Market Agency has so far not had a legal basis for applying the European Securities and Markets Authority guidelines and recommendations. This amendment will make the guidelines binding on the supervisory authorities as they will be made available through the Official Journal of the Republic of Slovenia. It's the other way around. Cooperation in the European financial control system shall be regulated and it shall be transferred to the national legal order that the competent authority shall, in carrying out its tasks, take care of the convergence of supervisory tools and supervisory practices in the application of laws. On the basis of that competent authority, in accordance with the principle of loyal cooperation set out in the Treaty on European Union, it shall cooperate in a spirit of trust and full mutual respect, in particular in providing relevant and reliable information on the movement between the competent authorities of the Member States. It shall also participate in the activities of the European Banking Authority and, where appropriate, the College of Supervisors. It's like a third. This is an area added for the sake of greater global connectivity and electronic commerce. Investors shall have access to markets in any country using computers or other electronic devices from their home country. In order for the competent authority to exercise control over the actions of investors from their country, they need information from the markets in which they are trading, otherwise they cannot successfully conduct the control procedure. In order to safeguard the integrity of the financial markets, the supervisory authorities have merged with an international organisation that brings together the supervisory authorities of the countries of all continents. They help each other to exercise control over financial markets through the provision of information, so the articles of the proposed law allow the Securities Market Agency to obtain the information it needs in supervisory procedures and guides them in order to protect the integrity of the financial markets and to provide such information. Only through the successful exchange of information can the supervisory authorities of countries successfully protect capital markets. In line with the above-mentioned and written additions to the provisions of the Law on the Market in Financial Instruments, as stated, this means the transposition of the three directives and the two regulations of the EU, the modern centre Party Group assesses the necessity of implementing this European legislation into the Slovenian legal order and will therefore support the proposal for a law amending and supplementing the Law on the Market in Financial Instruments.

ParlaMint-SI_2016-03-29-SDZ7-Redna-17.ana.u44	Thank you for your word and a nice greeting also on behalf of the Group of Social Democrats, where we will support the proposal of the Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Law on the Market in Financial Instruments. This issue has also been addressed to a large extent by the European Parliament in its work with several resolutions adopted both before and during the financial crisis. He called for more related European surveillance to ensure a truly level playing field for all participants at Union level. The European Council also recommended in its conclusions, following its meeting on 18 and 19 June 2009, the establishment of a European system of financial supervisors, to be composed of three new European supervisory authorities. It also recommended that the system should improve the quality and coherence of national controls, strengthen the control of cross-border groups and establish a uniform European rules to be applied to all financial institutions in the internal market. He stressed that the European supervisory authorities should also have powers to control credit rating agencies, and called on the Commission to make concrete proposals on how the European financial stability system could play an important role in crisis situations. These are the orientations that have been addressed both in the European Parliament and in the European Council and, consequently, for a very long time, that is, in 2008, 2009 this debate in the European Parliament and the European Council started, well, in 2016 we are implementing, if I may say so, these orientations also in national legislation in Slovenia. The main solutions that this law brings are, in particular, improving internal controls and increasing fines. Thus, investment firms must establish stable internal management arrangements, including a clear organisational composition, risk management procedures and appropriate internal control mechanisms. The provisions of the law also apply to banks if they provide investment services, but of course the Bank of Slovenia also controls banks in this area. However, the tighter penalties for violators are imposed by the Securities Market Agency, which may impose a fine on the stock exchange house up to twice the amount of the profits obtained by the infringement and 10 % of the total annual turnover. The fines for members of administrations and supervisory councils are also small, reaching up to €5 million or twice the amount created by the infringement. Fines limited to EUR 2 million are also provided for individuals who abuse the rules of the financial instruments market. These are the main indicators of the content of this law in order to try to establish as much control and responsibility as possible in the functioning of financial markets. The bill also tightens the rules that motivate business leaders to achieve short-term results. This removes the obligation of an interim management report and thus motivates investors to think in the long term. Some administrative obstacles are also being reduced to companies, introducing a single extended deadline for the publication of semi-annual accounting reports to 3 months after the end of the reporting period, and the law provides for even greater transparency in the payment of investments and for the harmonisation of the conditions for a central clearing company, in accordance with the European Regulation. Important novelties are also brought by law in the field of prospectuses for investors. The new rules provide for a clearer definition of persons responsible for correctness and completeness of information on prospectuses and also lay down the obligation to send final information on the prospectus to the European Securities and Markets Authority. We are confident that all these measures will have a very good impact on the further development of financial markets, and therefore we support this law. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2016-03-29-SDZ7-Redna-17.ana.u48	Thank you very much, Mr. President. Dear colleagues and colleagues, distinguished Madam Secretary of State! However, they did not bring the burdens and incentives most needed in this situation in Slovenia. Members of New Slovenia we are very much on the ground, on our regular monthly field visits throughout Slovenia. We visit economists, societies, local communities. From these visits we draw suggestions for our political action. We also see how legislation works in the field, in life, or how it does not work. We closely monitor the effects of the introduction of tax boxes and the first result of our findings is the novel which is before you, colleagues and colleagues. We are counting today on a constructive debate and on listening to arguments in the government or coalition and supporting the solutions that we are proposing, or perhaps even improving them. So we already have an analysis, and we have a solution. The proposed law pursues the objective of regulating the situation of voluntary firefighting companies and small taxpayers in relation to the use of online tax box lines, namely, for these two categories of taxpayers an exemption from the mandatory use of online cash in cash transactions. These debtors will, however, continue to conduct their cash operations in the same way as has been regulated by cash transactions so far, i.e. through so-called virtual tax registers or through a book of accounts, in accordance with the provisions of the applicable Tax Procedure Act. The second objective of the bill is to properly regulate the position or obligations of customers or consumers with regard to the taking and storing of accounts and the submission of the invoice to the authorised person. The main innovation brought by our law is to provide exemptions from the obligation to use online tax warehouses for two categories of entities, namely voluntary fire companies and small taxpayers. The latter include those who have not exceeded or are likely not to exceed EUR 50 000 per year in the last 12 months, whose annual tax turnover is governed by the rules governing value added tax. For these two categories, the current regime for mandatory use of online tax warehouses is an excessive administrative and financial burden. We all appreciate the sacrificial work of firefighters and firefighters, who, in the event of natural disasters, also risk their lives to help fellow citizens save property and save lives. In our view, it is right to make it easier for them to do business in a way that will allow them to devote more and better to their training and training tasks. The proposal follows the principles of proportionality and administrative simplicity. That is why we propose to abolish the fine for a buyer who does not submit an invoice at the request of an authorised person. We believe that the purpose of the law is not to punish or at least not to punish, but to establish a new tax culture, which can be achieved equally effectively or even more effectively only with a reminder. Members and Members of the New Slovenia today expect a constructive debate and I believe that, with common powers, with a rational approach, taking into account what is going wrong today with the implementation of the Law on tax clearance of accounts, we can do something good for our citizens, especially those who are in debt. It's not good to wait a year for some scientific analysis. We've already got the tests. Anyone who walks with his eyes open around the world, through Slovenia, knows what goes wrong. And there's a lot of things going wrong with the Law on tax clearance.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-03-29-SDZ7-Redna-17.ana.u49	For an introductory explanation of the opinion, I give the floor to the representative of the Government, State Secretary, Ministry of Finance mag. Matei Vranictor Erman.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-03-29-SDZ7-Redna-17.ana.u50	Mr President, thank you for the floor. Dear Members and Members! When drafting the law on tax clearance of accounts, we provided, in accordance with the requirements of the interested public at the time of public consideration of the proposal, that the obligation to tax confirmation of accounts was imposed on all debtors who were obliged to issue invoices in accordance with the rules on value added tax and to accept payments in cash, except for narrow exceptions. The system of tax attestation of invoices has been applied as a requirement since 2 January of this year, so that a comprehensive analysis and evaluation of the functioning of the system and its effects will not be possible until 2017. Why? This year we can analyse the effects of the value added tax calculation, but for a comprehensive analysis it is also necessary to take into account the calculation of the activity tax for natural persons and the calculation of the corporate income tax for legal persons to be submitted only in the following year. The fact is that this part of the analysis will be more negligible. Key analyses are based on VAT accounts that are possible in May 2016. Why? As a result of the fact that the first accounts will be submitted by the end of April for the period January-March 2016, the three-month VAT liability will be issued. The proposal to exempt taxable persons with annual tax turnover of less than EUR 50 000 would constitute an exemption from precisely those taxpayers who are generally more likely to receive cash for the payment of goods and services and, as such, are the main addressees of the tax clearance obligation. In 2014 in Slovenia, among all taxpayers for corporate income tax and income income from the activities of 70% of taxable persons who did not exceed EUR 50 000 of annual tax transport would be exempt from the obligation to tax the accounts. This obligation would be lifted by almost half of all the debtors currently included in the system of tax clearance of accounts. This would make the system in place lose its purpose and make a concrete analysis of the existing situation impossible. The achievement of the basic objectives of the law, namely the prevention of the grey economy, the detection of labour violations and the black and rationalisation of the operation of the tax authority by establishing a risk analysis system, would thus be impossible to implement the law proposal. The second part of the amendment concerns the exemption of fire companies and the applicant does not explain in any way why fire companies should be exempt from the obligation to confirm accounts. The question is whether in this case the constitutional condition of justification for the different treatment of non-essentially different debtors is met. With regard to the proposed abolition of the fine in the event that the buyer does not take the account, it should be pointed out that the buyer has an important role to play in preventing the grey economy, and the measure against which the proposed amendment opposes it is only one of the measures taken by the Financial Administration to promote awareness of the importance of issuing and taking accounts. It is also irrelevant that the issue of a reminder is possible and is also principal in the existing legal text. Your Honor! In view of all the above, the Government considers that the proposal for a law is not suitable for further consideration and we propose not to support it further. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2016-03-29-SDZ7-Redna-17.ana.u52	Mr President, thank you for saying so. Dear representatives of the Ministry of Finance, proposer, colleagues and colleagues! The Law on tax clearance of accounts was passed on from the SMC pre-election programme to the coalition contract and from there to last July. We consider it an important step towards limiting the grey economy and ensuring a level playing field between the debtors. At present, we do not have more detailed information to suggest a prudent introduction of any improvements, so the changes made by the applicant cannot be supported at this stage. The proposer specifically highlights three areas: fire companies, debtors reaching up to EUR 50 000 and a criminal provision relating to buyers. Let's start at the first. Voluntary fire companies shall be financed to a large extent by voluntary contributions and donations from legal and natural persons after writing to the applicants. It should be reiterated here that the current Account Certification Act does not provide for additional liability of the debtors in the case of grants, memberships or grants. For such transactions, accounts are generally not issued and, consequently, there is no so-called fiscalisation. Nor is the tax clearance of accounts in cases where funds or payments are made from the budget of the State, municipalities and various organisations, as these are generally indicated directly from the transaction account, i.e. the donor, the sponsor or the holder of the tender, to the beneficiary’s transaction account. The purpose of the law on tax clearance of accounts was not to restrict or make humanitarian or charitable activities more difficult, nor did it intervene. We have focused only on the commercial part of the activity and improving tax control. Therefore, as already mentioned, in the case of grants, memberships and grants, the treatment is the same as before, also in the case of sponsorships and comparable payments for services when they are transferred directly from the payer’s transaction account to the transaction account of a voluntary firefighter company. The change has therefore taken place in that part of the activities of voluntary fire companies, which are of a commercial nature and compete in the market, for example firejoys and other occasional events, with which companies, inter alia, provide additional funds for their operations. In such cases, the obligation to issue invoices was in force prior to the implementation of the Law on tax clearance of accounts, and this is by no means a novelty. The key difference is that, from now on, the accounts are either automatically validated in real-time financial administration at the same time as the issuance, either using the bound account book and subsequent validation. And now, more specifically, to organise the firejoys that the proposer specifically points out. In addition to the automatic controls on the issuance of accounts carried out on the financial management server, the tax clearance of the accounts has undoubtedly created some additional pressure to improve transparency and the organisation of transactions within the debtors themselves and in the case of associations, also in relation to their members. Inevitably, hundreds or thousands of visitors to a happy event lead to a high frequency of cash transactions linked to admissions, food, drink and sale of many other items. In the light of the diversified chain of stakeholders crucifying from catering service providers, entertainment programme providers, sound, lighting, stage, etc., it is reasonable to expect the State to ensure that the entire cash flow and thus the payment chain of fees, services and goods are properly recorded. It would probably be irrational for firefighters to have all the infrastructure to carry out firelights, which are usually organised once a year and in the practice of society provide the bulk of equipment only at such events, from benches, tables, stage, sound, lights to bar. The same applies to the establishment and operation of a central sales post at an event where, normally, one or more cash registers sell coupons or cards that can then be cashed for services or goods at the event, since it is difficult in practice, without the central control of the organiser over the cash flow, to provide a fundamental rule in the business that must be provided for each time properly cooperation with different stakeholders. Clean accounts — good friends if we sum this part up. The rules for voluntary fire companies have not changed the rules for voluntary fire companies by adopting the Tax attestation Act in the main part of the operation relating to membership fees, grants, grants and transactions from the transaction account to the transaction account. However, for a part of the business, which is by nature a market activity, the reasons mentioned by the proposer only cheaply do not justify the introduction of a distinction in the exercise of control over the conduct of cash traffic. It is a fact that the activities of societies or institutions would be difficult to separate from activities of parts or other legal forms, as there is a frequent overlap between profitable and non-profit activities. On the one hand, small voluntary firefighters may be companies, on the other, perhaps Auto-moto links with their companies, to third different companies for the organisation of events, to the fourth capital company involved. The range therefore ranges from some 100 euros to several millions and activities from one to countless. Therefore, the unilateral and partial exemption of a particular type of organisation or activity could inevitably lead to a distortion of conditions between different organisations. The time has passed when some of the hosts, as well as some other commercial activities, were operating within societies or institutions and thus unfairly competing with the usual guesthouses. A major change in this area has already been made by the Ministry of Finance, which was adopted a good decade ago. This has also drawn a clearer line between profitable and non-profit activities, regardless of what the surplus or profit is spent on. By enforcing the poorly thought-out exceptions that we are considering today, we could get help from those who would like to play by the rules rather than those who would like to relieve them. And if we put the proposal directly from theory into practice. Perhaps the next concert of the world-form musician in Stožice would have been organised as a fire news by the NSI Members, at the confirmation of the proposal. Furthermore, the proposer strengthens the application of the Law on tax clearance of invoices to those whose turnover exceeds EUR 50 000 per year. There are several problems and uncertainties in this regard. In this group there are a number of risk debtors addressed by the Law on tax clearance of accounts. The automaticisation of the control work of these debtors allows the financial administration to focus its data analysis solely on those debtors who, on the basis of concrete data, prove to be potential violators. This makes it possible to significantly improve the organisation of the work of inspection services and the lower burden on those debtors who operate in an exemplary manner. The financial management could also, by means of a new system, increase the number of inspectors on the ground to verify the unfair competition represented by the rainforests. There was a lot of talk about these at the time of the adoption of the Law on tax clearance of accounts, and this can now be addressed more easily and better by inspectors who were previously overloaded by the control of treasury traffic in a way that can be substantially faster and more comprehensively carried out by Furs' server. When reviewing the structure and number of issues and initiatives, it can also be observed that a number of debtors who should have made their accounts in order to carry out at least part of their activities so far have not done so in accordance with the legislation in force. Indeed, it is often true that the Law on tax clearance of accounts for certain activities has established the obligation to issue invoices, which of course it does not. The issue of invoices is largely governed by other rules, such as the Value Added Tax Act, which were adopted by the end of the 1990s. The law on tax clearance of accounts governs only what it contains on behalf, namely the validation of accounts, namely those issued by the debtor in the course of a cash transaction. In the last few words about penalties for customers. The penalty is merely symbolic to a certain extent for the buyer, since in the case of a sentence it amounts to EUR 20 if it is settled within eight days. The inspector also has the opportunity to give a warning under the Code of Criminal Procedure. This one has no financial implications for the buyer. According to the data available for once, inspectors have been given mainly warnings or warnings so far. In the first months of its entry into force, they have, as announced, taken the role of assistants to the taxpayers in fulfilling their tax obligations in terms of referring to changes and have thus taken an additional step towards building a future relationship between the debtors and the financial administration, which is increasingly looking at the debtor as a partner. At present, the more precise effects brought by the tax collectors are still a little premature to talk about, as data are only available for the monthly VAT debtors for January 2016. For February, they are expected in the coming days; in mid-May, data will be available for the three-month VAT debtors, which will certainly allow for a significantly better insight into tax effects. The last important package of data will only be available in April 2017, as the data of those who are not taxable for VAT purposes will then be available and only annual reports will be submitted to the Slovenian Public Records Agency. The counter on the financial administration's websites shows the following picture: the number of digital certificates taken is almost 70,000, the number of tax-confirmed accounts is almost 250 million and so on. For the time being, it is only possible to conclude from this data that the system set up by the Financial Administration worked well. Response times are measured, as promised, in hundreds of seconds. A free mini-tax box was established and a number of initiatives have been put in place for buyers and debtors, ranging from prizes to implementation aid and a large media awareness campaign, the integral part of which is aid provided by tax inspectors on the ground. So there is no more accurate data. However, the first indications already show that the system has been set up justly and that it works effectively. Also a good year and a half applies a transitional period which allows the use of the bound account book. In the SMC Group, we will not support the proposal of a group of NSI members for the reasons presented. Thank you.

Taking into account the purpose of the law, this is to prevent a grey economy, then we cannot punish the buyer for something that the seller is primarily obliged to do. However, we believe and assess that the grey and black economies cannot be effectively suppressed by tax receipts, because those who do not want to issue invoices will not in the future, either, but we can effectively pursue the grey and black economies by reducing tax burdens and taxes.

ParlaMint-SI_2016-03-29-SDZ7-Redna-17.ana.u68	Thank you very much. Allow me to have a few responses to the claims and views put forward so far. The system of clearance of accounts for tax purposes has practically only come to life. One of the key things we see this year, when we meet more intensively with representatives of economic operators or their interest associations, is the call for tax legislation not to change so often. The unpredictability of rapid changes is one of the key problems for the entities we have talked to. The same applies to the system of clearance of accounts. When we talk about checking out accounts, we are not talking about bills to pay taxes on, but we are talking about bills to pay for cash. Therefore, it is not appropriate to combine the obligation to confirm an account with the obligation to pay a tax, as proposed in relation to non-governmental organisations. In fact, the idea that NGOs should pay flat-rate taxes on their activities puts them in a worse position than they do today, when incomes that are not considered to be income-generating activities can be exempt from taxation and can only be taxed on revenue and expenditure relating to taxable activities. Liabilities that only occasionally arrange or carry out an activity in respect of which it is necessary to provide tax attestation of invoices, have the option under the applicable law or to secure a tax box, may use the tax box available on the website of the Financial Administration to hire a cash register if they manage this activity only for a short period of time, may, in essence, issue accounts by means of a linked account book and subsequently confirm such accounts. The options are considerable and can choose between these and thus optimize the costs incurred in this regard. Let me point out that our assessment that the costs for each taxpayer amount to around EUR 300 per tax box and that this assessment was applied when the law was passed, according to our data, has also been confirmed. The obligation to issue accounts shall arise in cases where an account is to be issued. When we talk about the smallest debtors, we need to ask ourselves whether the administrative burden for these smallest debtors is actually due, this is a confirmation of the account, whether the administrative burden to be addressed is already the issue of the account itself. This is the direction that we are thinking about at the Ministry of Finance regarding the resolution of the open problems that we face in the first months of the validation of accounts. So it's a question of exactly where do we address the problem, whether for the consequences or for the very reason. The question was raised when the first analyses would be made. In my opening statement, I mentioned that the analyses relating to taxpayers may be prepared after the first invoice is issued for those taxable persons whose tax period is three months, so for the period January-March 2016, the accounts will be submitted by the end of April of this year, meaning that in May they could have made the first analysis. But already the accounts for January show a different picture than was presented in today's discussions. It is true that the figure published in relation to the implementation of the value added tax in February is slightly lower than in last year, but this is only the final profit, which does not take into account that in February there were many more returns to the value added tax due to overpayments than in last year and that payments increased by around 4 % in February compared to last February. These are all payments made by VAT debtors, month-old taxpayers for VAT. If we exclude from this analysis those taxpayers who issue or carry out an activity where more frequently or mainly invoices are issued or pay services and goods in cash, then we can find that the net increase in January and February was more than 7 %, which means high, above average growth. I do not wish to argue at this point that this is due to the introduction of tax boxes, because, as I mentioned above, it is too early to make such claims. However, this information is an important indicator that we can be satisfied with the analysis of the effects of the tax registers I have previously announced. Two more data points in the same direction. We know that the validation of accounts is important not only from the point of view of paying taxes but also from the point of view of combating work and black employment. And if we compare the volume of new regular employment in micro and small taxpayers for full-time and part-time work, we find that in January 2015 this number of regular employees increased by a thousand 400 or 17%. The same is true for the month of February. In the number of new regular jobs for part-time work, this number is even higher. The index in January was 317 in February 247. These are, once again, indications pointing us in the direction that we have achieved the purpose we have pursued with the tax box, although I do not wish to argue that all these results are the result of the tax box. We are still preparing detailed analyses of these data that I have presented. Furthermore, the reduction or question raised with regard to the liquidation of debtors carrying out less than EUR 50 000 per year and at the same time by fire companies. Fire companies are also liable for less than €50,000 of annual tax transport, but more than half or about half of all taxpayers represent those who are voluntarily involved in the VAT system and their annual tax turnover amounts to less than €50,000. I therefore wonder whether this particular exposure to fire companies actually means that these fire companies carry out more than 50,000 euros of annual taxation. Neither the Ministry nor the Financial Administration have ever advocated this; even more, we have pointed out that, as far as a solution is being developed towards the recommended donations and certain goods are being delivered for that, let's say quasi donation, that is also the transport of goods or services. Because of this, as I mentioned in the introduction, we cannot only think about whether certain debtors should be excluded from the system of clearance of accounts, but we must ask ourselves whether it makes sense that accounts for certain traffic in goods and services are issued at all, and this is not a matter of this law, but of other rules. In this context, there are also questions relating to complementary activities on the farm and in relation to machines. In addition to the assessment of the number of debtors for the validation of accounts. Last year, we prepared an assessment of potential debtors for the validation of accounts based on business register data, taking into account the main activity following the standard classification of activities where we have seen a higher cash payment in the past periods. These are so-called potential pledges. These are the people who were in the process of drafting the law, according to our estimates of between 60 and 80 thousand. For the time being, the number of registered debtors certifying the accounts does not mean that only half of the debtors are actually included in the system of checking the accounts and that the others are not issuing the invoices at all, but that it was actually an assessment that was made on the basis of the data available at the time. Today, in fact, we can see that as many as 51,610 pledges have already taken over digital certificates, 7 thousand and 800 are those who issue bound accounts and subsequently confirm them. So we are gradually approaching the estimated number of potential debtors. However, the fact is that, as you have also noted in your debates, not all debtors operate regularly, but some also have occasional traffic in goods and services for cash, so we can expect the number to increase in the coming months to a certain extent. In view of the fact that the first assessment was made on the basis of very general data, we cannot ignore the possibility that we will not go through that lower number than the estimated possibilities, this is to go beyond the figures of 60,000 debtors, which would also somehow be in line with what has been taken so far by digital certificates. So much for now. Thank you.
