The draft law was tabled by a group of 5 Members and Members with a first-signed mag. Matthew Tonin. With regard to this bill, the group of 26 Members and Members with their first signed magi is a group of 26 Members. Matthew Tonin demanded that the National Assembly hold a general discussion. In order to further explain the proposal, I give the floor to the representative of the proposer, Mr Joseph Horvat.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-03-29-SDZ7-Redna-17.ana.u138	Thank you for your word, Madam Vice President. Madam Minister with co-workers, dear colleagues and colleagues! We Christian Democrats have prepared a new Law on Health Care and Health Insurance, which fairly regulates the regressive claims that the Institute of Health Insurance of Slovenia applies from employers in case of injury to work or occupational disease. Regret claims or claims for damages arising from an accident at work or occupational disease or against persons responsible for the damage caused shall be submitted in accordance with Slovenian legislation by the Institute for Health Insurance of Slovenia and the Institute for Pension and Disability Insurance of Slovenia. The obliging law of the Republic of Slovenia defines liability as an obligation for an individual to settle the damage that he is responsible for and which arises only if four conditions are met – inadmissibility, damage, causation and liability. Indemnity liability for health insurance is more precisely regulated by the Health and Health Insurance Act. The Slovenian Institute of Health Insurance has the right to request from the employer compensation for damage to the amounts paid if the injury, illness or death of the worker is due to the failure of the employer to take appropriate measures for the safe working environment and the safety of persons. The bond law provides that the court, having regard to the property situation of the injured person, may order the responsible person to pay a lesser compensation than the amount of the damage if the damage was not caused on purpose and not for serious negligence, and that the responsible person is of weak property status and would, at the time of payment of the full compensation, settle in the absence of compensation. New Slovenia's proposal regulates employers' claims in a way that they are responsible for injury at work and occupational disease only in the event of gross negligence, while in other cases the costs are reimbursed by the Slovenian Institute of Health Insurance, where employers pay their contributions. The purpose of the safe and healthy work regime is to prevent injuries at work and occupational diseases and to ensure social security for those who are injured at work or who are sick at work for a occupational disease, but at the same time in a predictable legal and business environment that does not endanger the existence of the cause. Christian Democrats feel that the current legislation is unfair, especially to a small employer who answers unlimitedly. Self-employed entrepreneurs, even with their personal property, even though a damaging event with high material damage has been caused by some small oversight of an obligation that is able to detect little negligence. Indemnification claims may jeopardise the business of some employers, particularly those who are economically weaker, small and medium-sized. Nor can employers be able to protect the risk associated with the regression claims from commercial insurance companies because they do not offer these services. In 2013, the Slovenian Institute of Health Insurance recorded an inflow of EUR 213 000 249 in 2013 and EUR 456 thousand 110 in 2014 and EUR 176 thousand 901 in 2015. In New Slovenia, we therefore propose that the employer's liability is only in case of occupational illness, injury at work or death due to gross negligence on the part of the employer or on purpose, whereas in the event of low negligence, the regression requirement is not enforced, as all employers pay a contribution of 0.53% in case of injury at work and occupational illness in the case of compulsory health insurance. According to the Slovenian Institute of Health Insurance, the revenue from this title was EUR 73 million in 2014 and EUR 74 million in 2015, which is used to finance all health programmes. Employers' responsibility for compensation is limited to 20 times, as proposed by the amendment, if the damage is caused by great negligence, and unlimited if the damage is intentionally caused. The restriction is to avoid jeopardising the existence of a cause of injury, especially by small and medium-sized employers, as it does not allow for their stable and predictable operations. I'm concluding. The New Slovenia proposal does not go any way of reducing the importance of safety and health at work, nor does it reduce the employer's standards of responsibility, but more equitable burden sharing among all stakeholders. Thank you very much, and I apologize for the time lapse.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-03-29-SDZ7-Redna-17.ana.u139	For an introductory explanation, I give the floor to the representative of the Government, Minister of Health Maria Milojka Kolar Celarc.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-03-29-SDZ7-Redna-17.ana.u140	Honourable President of the National Assembly, honourable Members of Parliament, distinguished Members of Parliament! The Government of the Republic of Slovenia received in the opinion of the National Assembly a proposal for a law amending and supplementing the Law on Health Care and Health Insurance, submitted to the National Assembly by a group of Members and Members with the first-signed Mr Mag. Matthew Tonin. The Government of the Republic of Slovenia fully opposes the Proposal for a Law amending and supplementing the Law on Health Care and Health Insurance, which amends the fundamental principle of compensation law in order to compensate the person who caused the damage at the level caused. In our opinion, the bill also opposes the basic principles of the Law on Safety and Health at Work and also interferes with the fundamental obligations of the Labour Relations Act. The rules in force are based on the fundamental principle of compensation law in order to compensate the person who caused the damage at the level caused. The proposal does not follow the principle mentioned above, as it proposes an arrangement that the Slovenian Institute of Health Insurance would have the right to request from the employer the damage caused only when the disease, injury or death of the insured person was caused by the employer’s intention or serious negligence to suspend the implementation of safety or health measures at work, appropriate sanitary measures or other measures prescribed or prescribed for the safety of persons. Furthermore, the proposal further limits the employer's responsibility by proposing to limit the amount of damage caused in the event of gross negligence to the 20th timer of the lowest pension base. The compensation obligation is of a civil nature and therefore the purpose is not to penalise the responsible person, but to establish the situation prior to the occurrence of the injury. Thus, the primary function of compensation law is to eliminate the unfair situation in which the injured person was found, namely, to eliminate his deprivation or injury. This is the so-called countervailing function of compensation law. The draft law follows only partially the principles of compensation, but only when the disease, injury or death of the worker was caused by the intentional abandonment of the implementation of safety or health measures at work and limits compensation liability in the event of serious negligence and completely forgets compensation in the event of light negligence, even in spite of the fulfilment of the general assumptions of the civilian offence, that is the wrongfulness of the conduct of the damage. The proposed solution reduces the importance of existing legislation in the field of safety and health at work if it does not completely destroy it. As proposed, it means that in the field of safety and health at work, the standard of employer responsibility would be substantially reduced. At the same time, such arrangements would be contrary to other principles of protection at work, as provided for in Article 5 of the Law on Protection and Safety and Health at Work and to promote a working relationship. The employer is a professional in a particular activity and must therefore be aware of all safety measures and therefore be subject to stricter criteria than the average person or employee who is the weaker party in this case. In the light of what the Government of the Republic of Slovenia has said, it reiterates that it does not support the proposal for a law submitted to the National Assembly by a group of Members and Members, since the proposal therefore opposes the rules of compensation for employers' liability, the rules of protection and health at work and the obligations of the employer arising from the working relationship. Thank you.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-03-29-SDZ7-Redna-17.ana.u141	We are moving on to a presentation on the views of the groups of Members. The text is given by the Slovenian Democratic Party's Parliamentary Group, Mrs Nada Brinovšek.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-03-29-SDZ7-Redna-17.ana.u142	Thank you for your word, President. Nicely greeted colleagues? Greetings, Your Honor! The Health and Health Insurance Act provides that the Institute of Health Insurance of Slovenia may apply a claim for compensation in the event of injury at work from those who intentionally or negligently caused health damage to the insured person, it is said, from employers in the event that the disease, injury or death of the insured person was caused by a lack of adequate hygiene measures, occupational health measures or other measures designed for the safety of persons. In particular, the law highlights the employer’s responsibility in the event that the employer does not provide real data or intentionally violates legislation. The provisions of the law also relate to the liability of an employee in the event of an offence committed. The law also provides that the institution has the right to demand that all the damage caused be compensated by the employer. According to existing legislation, such compensation claims may jeopardise the business of certain employers. This is mainly the case for economically weaker employers, so to speak, small and medium-sized enterprises, because they are not able to secure the risk associated with the regression claims in commercial insurance companies because these insurance services are not insured. In view of the changes and additions to the law, the employer's liability is proposed only if the employer violates the rules on purpose or because of gross negligence. Where the cause of injury on the part of the worker or in cases where negligence is easier, the employer should not be liable. The proposed solution therefore excludes the employer's liability in the event of his ease of negligence and limitation of liability when the damage is caused by the worker's negligence. The employer would therefore answer if the damage was done on purpose or if it was serious negligence. However, light negligence would be a burden of compulsory health insurance, where employers pay a special contribution to injuries at work and occupational diseases. The Government of the Republic of Slovenia, in its opinion on this point, stated that it is, of course, opposed to the proposal for amendments to this Act, as it is changing the fundamental principle of compensation law, which provides that damage at the level caused by it will be reimbursed by the person who has just caused it. It is true that the employer must in all respects ensure the safety and health of workers in relation to work, but it is well known that both the employer and the worker face unforeseen circumstances in their work, which are not under the control of employers and workers. Sometimes such events cannot be avoided. For example, a worker who is under severe pressure to reach his standard is, of course, also partially under stress, somewhat overlooked in his work process, causing so-called lax negligence at work. So in this case, the employer will be punished. However, when it comes to serious injury at work, which can cause permanent consequences, disability, or even death, the cost of treatment may be very high. Also high are the workers' costs of absence from work, which can last even after a month or more. And such a request can reach up to €80,000, and in a number of cases it can reach more. In the event of disability, however, we should not forget the life pension. Such requests may already endanger the business of a number of companies. The responsibility of individual independent entrepreneurs is particularly problematic as they are responsible for business obligations with all their personal property. The Government of the Republic of Slovenia also opposes the amendment of the law concerning the limitation of the amount of damage caused to 20 times the lowest pension base. The Government of the Republic of Slovenia believes that the current solution for determining the level of liability of compensation left to the court is correct. But we know what happens and how many times in our courts. The Slovenian Institute of Health Insurance, however, states in its letter to this point that anyone who causes harm to another is obliged to repay her if she fails to prove that the damage was caused without his fault, as the threat of compensation, of course, discourages her from misbehavior and employer's misbehavior. Here, I must admit, it raises the question of how many such cases there are in order for an employer or an employee to cause some damage on purpose. Of course, the Institute of Health Insurance of Slovenia does not provide data in its writing, or record cases of intentional mistreatment and how many of these cases there are, of course, if they are. I don't think there's much of a case like this. Mistreatment is mostly due to ignorance or in all too many cases due to overcrowding of workers. The Slovenian Institute of Health Insurance also states in its letter to this point that it recorded an inflow of 1.2 million in 2013, 1.4 million in 2014 and 1.2 million in 2015. It is interesting that the Ministry of Health replied to the question in response to the question raised by its colleague Mahnič, citing: "Looking at the business report of the Institute of Health Insurance of Slovenia, in 2014 this had 20.2 million revenue from the regression claims. The Slovenian Institute of Health Insurance has data for each debtor separately, but there is no data collected according to the status form.” End of quote. Dear colleagues! What kind of data should we believe now? Information from the Slovenian Institute of Health Insurance or data from the Ministry? We will, of course, support the proposed amendment of the law in the Slovenian Democratic Party. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-03-29-SDZ7-Redna-17.ana.u143	The text is given by the Group of the Democratic Party of the Pensioners of Slovenia, Mr Tomaž Gantar.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-03-29-SDZ7-Redna-17.ana.u144	Thank you very much, President. Dear Minister, colleagues and colleagues! Let me say in the introduction that the Desus Group will not support the amendment of the Health and Health Insurance Act. According to our assessment, the proposal of New Slovenia largely obliges employers to bear responsibility for the occurrence of injury, illness or death of an employee or insured person, as specified in the law, which is unacceptable for our parliamentary group. At the same time, it limits the bill's amount of compensation to 20 times the lowest pension base. New Slovenia's proposal goes in the opposite direction, as advocated by Desus, where we advocate a clearly defined responsibility of the employer, who must invest in the health of his employees by ensuring consistent protection at work and providing favourable working conditions, which ultimately leads to greater satisfaction in the workplace, lower sickness, fewer injuries at work, fewer hospital stocks and also greater productivity. However, in the proposal for a novelty, the employer would be obliged to pay for the damage caused in any form whatsoever only in cases where it was caused by the employer's gross negligence or where it was caused on purpose. However, the question arises as to who will be able to prove to the employers that it was, for example, great and not minor negligence or that it was caused on purpose. On the other hand, the practice on the other hand all too often shows that workers are blamed for accidents at work, while employers are often held accountable. In fact, in the Desus Group, we are surprised by the proposal's approach to this law, because the proposal clearly protects the employer at the expense of the worker's safety, which, in relation to the employer, is weaker and in a subordinate position, while the employer largely detracts from the responsibility for causing injury, illness or death of the worker in the performance of his duties. In such a proposal, it is difficult to detect the values that are often referred to by the novel proposal. The new Slovenia is deceiving the fact that the law will have no financial and no other consequences. This is something that we cannot agree with in our parliamentary group, because if we look at the data of the Health Insurance Office, it recorded in the years 2013 to 2015 an inflow of between 1 million and almost 1 million euros. The reduction in the liability of employers would undoubtedly also reduce the flow of funds to the health treasury. Less money for a health-care register based on regression claims simply means less money for the implementation of health-care programmes from public funds and more from the pockets of patients, which I hope is not the point. The draft law will also have effects on the economy and the social sector, contrary to what the novel's applicants have said, because we are convinced that the proposal does not follow the principle of fairness, as the motioner claims. Nor does the proposal of the novel protect the right to personal dignity and security, the inviolability of the human body and mental integrity and the right to a healthy living environment. The draft law opposes the fundamental principles of safety and health at work, as it changes the basis of the compensation law, so that the cause of the damage in the amount caused should be reimbursed. For one more reason, we can also agree with the Government's view that the proposal for a law is not suitable for further consideration. The limitation of compensation liability and compensation gives employers the wrong message about the obligation to establish and ensure safety and health at work. As we believe in the Group of Members that the proposal for a new law sets the economic interests of employers high above ensuring the protection and health of workers, we will vote in the Group of Members, as I have already mentioned, that the proposal is not suitable for further consideration. Thank you.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-03-29-SDZ7-Redna-17.ana.u145	The word is given by the Group of Social Democrats, for them mag. Bojana Mursich.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-03-29-SDZ7-Redna-17.ana.u146	Thank you very much, President for your word. A respected minister with a team, colleagues and colleagues! Today we are considering one of two substantively identical proposals for the amendment of the law, which was tabled in the parliamentary procedure by New Slovenia – Christian Democrats. We'll deal with one today, one on Thursday. Both novels are contentually summarising the proposals of the Chamber of Commerce regarding the so-called regression claims. Allow me to briefly summarize what the proposer wants to achieve by amending the Health and Health Insurance Act. Indemnification claims or regression claims have recently been a common topic faced by employers. Therefore, through their associations and chambers, they advocate amending the rules under which the Institute for Health Insurance of Slovenia would recover the damage caused by the cost of treatment, payment of salaries and other costs due to damage to the workplace. Employers point out that these claims may endanger their business and require that employers and employees share responsibility for injuries at work. The health insurance company does not check the liability of employees for injury in the workplace, and employers may prove the fault of the employee in the proceedings. Nor can employers be able to protect the risk associated with the regression claims from commercial insurance companies because they do not offer these services. With regard to this content, the other party points out that the purpose of the regression claims is by no means to charge the medical bills, but rather to repay the funds paid for the fault of someone else, i.e. in the event of damage at work from the employer who has breached or abandoned measures to ensure the safe work of workers. It adds that regression claims even force employers to take care of the working environment and protect workers from threats. The Slovenian Institute of Health Insurance has joined the intensive enforcement and, of course, the recovery of regressive claims for treatment costs due to injuries at work. This is also followed by the Institute for Pension and Disability Insurance of Slovenia, which requires the reimbursement of damage caused by the employer, if the insured person’s disability, the need for foreign assistance and service or death is due to the failure to carry out safety and health measures at work and other measures prescribed or prescribed for the safety of persons. Employers expose the mania of the ceiling for compensation and also that there is no distinction between whether any damage was caused by negligence or by chance, which they consider to be a heavy burden, especially for small businesses and craftsmen. On the other hand, we are faced with arguments from the opposite side that, if we were to limit the amount of regressive claims, this damage caused by the employer through his inappropriate conduct and beyond a specific border would burden the tax box and thus solidarity of all contributors, including those employers who consistently and exemplaryly implement all safety and health measures at work. It is clear from the above that it is a difficult walnut to crush by all the parties involved. In our view, this issue is often the subject of discussion in the Economic and Social Council, where, in our view, it also belongs. We believe that the solution must be the result of consensus between all stakeholders, so that I cannot agree on a unilateral amendment of legislation without agreement and we will vote in the Social Democrats' Group that the law is not suitable for further consideration.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-03-29-SDZ7-Redna-17.ana.u147	The word is given by the Group of the United Left for her by Miha Kordish.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-03-29-SDZ7-Redna-17.ana.u148	Thank you for your word, chairman. We will vote against this proposal in the United Left because it would reduce safety at work. It is our view that employers' responsibility for the safety and health of workers should not be reduced. We come from the conclusion that accidents are too much and that the situation in this area should never, by definition, be satisfied. In particular, we should not be satisfied in Slovenia, where the situation is not improving. The last report of the Labour Inspectorate shows that for the last three years, on average, 9 000 509 accidents per year have been reported, of which 439 are more severe, the number of accidents is even increasing. In 2014, 23 accidents ended with the death of the worker, the largest since the outbreak of the crisis in 2009. The Labour Inspectorate notes that it is necessary to quote: "The causes of accidents also in 2014 are mainly at a low level of safety culture for both employers and workers, risk underestimation and in some cases the inexperience of workers." End of quote. This finding needs to be complemented, the worker is always in a subordinate position and cannot organise the work process. The employer is also legally responsible for occupational safety training and it is clear that safety at work will only increase through the active efforts of employers. These measures generate certain costs, they spend some working time, so, of course, employers avoid them, saying they need to be forced into implementing security measures. The provision that the applicants would like to amend is designed precisely for this purpose, its main purpose is not to increase the revenue of the Health Insurance Institute, but rather to prevent the impact on employers. If this proposal were to be adopted, this preventive effect would be significantly reduced, resulting from all three proposed amendments. Let's go in order. Firstly, the proposal foresees that the employer would only be liable in compensation for the damage caused by gross negligence and therefore fall short of the liability for the damage caused by slight negligence. In this regard, it must be immediately said that the injuries caused by slight negligence are no easier than those caused by gross negligence. Therefore, this proposal does not address the liability of compensation for easier injuries, but removes liability for all types of injuries, and in principle for fatal ones. The difference between severe and malpractice is only that in severe negligence the employer is aware of the potential for damage. In the case of light negligence, however, he does not realize it, but he should be aware of it as a caring person. And that is precisely the purpose of all our labour law and labour protection legislation. If someone chooses to live off the work of others and is to set up a company for this purpose, they must take maximum care of safety at work. It's not enough to eliminate those dangers that every layman notices, but it has to eliminate all the dangers that can be predicted. If he can't do it, he's got to shut down the stall or he's got to shut down that stable. Secondly, the following amendment provides for a limitation of compensation in the event of damage due to gross negligence to a good EUR 15 000. One of the forces that forces the employer not to ignore the potential dangers is greatly favored by this. The definition of gross negligence is that the employer is aware of the danger, but still does not act. By reducing compensation liability, we encourage those employers who are aware of the dangers to ignore it on purpose. Such employers are common criminals to us. However, what NSI proposes is exactly the same as reducing penalties for violations of the rules on safety and health at work. We therefore oppose these measures in the United Left. The proponent in the explanatory memorandum promises some positive effects. The position of the United Left is that, with safety and health at work, baranting is not allowed. At the same time, it is noted that the applicant has misjudged the positive effects. The proposal states that the proposal will not have an impact on public finances. This is not true, of course, the purpose of these measures is to eliminate employers' compensation liability to the Health Insurance Institute. If this proposal were accepted, the institution would no longer have a basis for requiring compensation from the employer who caused the injury caused by light negligence, so the institution would have a lower income for this. In a letter dated 9 March, the ZZZS Governing Board reported that in the last three years, the company had obtained EUR 1.3 million per year through its regression applications. Thus, the law does not meet the business conditions for submission because it does not provide a real assessment of the negative effects on public finances, but at least this is deceiving, to say the least. It is true that EUR 1.3 million for the ZZZ is not a large amount, but this measure is mainly a preventive measure. At the same time, I would like to point out that the institution is already understaffed for austerity measures. By comparison, €1.3 million is the annual cost of 43 highly educated workers. Finally, I would like to point out that the scale of the institution's funds in terms of GDP is declining and will continue to decline due to the wrong policies of this government. Finally, I would like to touch on the claim that such an arrangement would primarily benefit small businesses. That's not true either. The proposal provides for a limitation of compensation liability for all employers, regardless of size. Otherwise, it is our view that there can be no positive discrimination against small businesses as regards safety at work. Anyone who wants to live off a foreign job has to follow all the standards or just shut the company down. Here we can help with analogy with environmental standards, the ban on the chlorination of meat or the use of growth hormones in Slovenia applies both to the largest multinational and to the smallest espionage or farmer. The same must be true of safety at work. We have no reason to support this law. Of all possible subsidies and tax reliefs, there is also a proposal for a measure which has a minimal financial impact on businesses while at the same time jeopardising the safety of all employees. I hope that the proposal would not agree with even the vast majority of the employers on whose behalf the applicants are speaking. The Health Insurance Office, by letter to the National Assembly on 9 March, informed the State that, again, I quote: "it fully opposes the proposed legal solution." End of quote. Like everyone here, you probably know, the office is run by a board of directors, where employers have four, along with representatives of farmers, five members of the 11th. This is a very clear signal that they do not agree with the health dumping policy. The SPIZ Council also rejected in December 2013 a similar limitation on the level of responsibility as proposed today. Therefore, the law before us does not reflect any real social interest, except the economically least productive sector, and only the applicant's poor judgment. Therefore, in our view, it is not suitable for further consideration. Thank you.

According to Slovenian legislation, the Slovenian Institute of Health Insurance has the right to claim compensation for damage if the injury, illness or death of the worker has occurred because the appropriate measures for protection and the proper working environment have not been implemented. There is, of course, no problem in this, that no one will feel that we are opposed to it. However, with the amendment of the Health and Health Insurance Act, where the law provides, arrangements would be restored in some way in 2003, namely that compensation from employers in the event of injury at work and occupational disease would be required if it was caused by gross negligence. In the event of minor negligence, however, and the damage would be recovered from compulsory health insurance, where employers pay a monthly contribution of 0,33 % for this purpose. Like I said, in any case, the employer pays for the injury. The law also limits the amount of compensation for the employer in the event of gross negligence, i.e. 20 times the lowest pension base, and I said that. Under the current legislation, in Slovenia, it is not possible to protect the employer's liability in the event of injury at work and occupational disease, as is possible in the case of car insurance in the event of accidents. Independent entrepreneurs are among the most vulnerable groups of employers who respond with their personal property even in the event of minor negligence. So much for some of you saying that this law is not meant for small craftsmen and entrepreneurs. That's what it's all about, because you're in the greatest danger. Big companies can afford a lot of things. In the event of an accident that may not end well and even if a worker may be responsible for it, this company may even be closed down. By amending the legislation we are proposing, we do not want to underline this, we do not want to give any solace and prejudice to the required rules for the safety and health of workers, but merely to distribute financial responsibility for safety and health at work in an appropriate manner. Each participant must assume his or her share of the responsibility. Also, solutions are not addressed to workers or to the equalisation of existing legislation on safety and health at work. It is only a fairer distribution of responsibility. In Slovenia, we cannot secure regressive liability with any insurance company, for example, craftsmen and entrepreneurs in neighbouring Austria and Germany. In particular, this means that they have a better business environment because of this option. It is in Slovenia that we have repeatedly been critical of the functioning of the ZZS, including the transparency of the financing of individual rights under the general agreement. We do not think it is fair that health contributions paid for the purpose of safety and health at work are lost in this common bag and are not deliberately used for injury to work or occupational disease. This means that those 0.54%, 0.53%, which is intended for injury at work, are virtually now in this health-related loss for anything. However, we propose that if employers pay for this purpose for injuries at work, then this insurance company should also cover these costs in case of injuries due to minor negligence. The New Slovenia Group did not change the provisions which would in any way interfere with intentional injury, but it is only right that the employer is fully responsible for the burden of causing damage, even if this means the cessation of the business. When it comes to intent when it comes to deliberately reducing workers' safety measures, we also advocate in Nova Slovenije that such a company is better closed today than tomorrow. The purpose of the amendments we propose and support is therefore not to reduce the importance of safety and health at work, but not to reduce the employer's standard of responsibility, but to divide the burden more justly among all stakeholders. Clearly, the marriage we have written will also be supported.

The Law on Health Care and Health Insurance seeks to regulate the area of employers' compensation claims in cases of injury at work. It is a request for reimbursement of the costs of treatment that the Slovenian Institute of Health Insurance may require of one which is intentionally or negligently causing health damage to an employee. In the case of various forms of injury at work or occupational disease, the employer is usually responsible, which also assumes liability under the bond and compensation law. This is not automaticism in the sense that it also means that the employer is guilty of damage. We are therefore aware of cases where the employer in the court has difficulty in proving that a certain damage was not caused by the negligence of the employer, but by the worker himself who is responsible for the accident at work. Since there is no ceiling for compensation in the payment of these regression claims and it is often very difficult to distinguish in the process of determining the responsible person from whether any damage was caused by negligence or by chance, employers point to the grey field in the system of labour law, which should be properly regulated. We are already familiar with the issue in the Task Force of the Modern Centre Party and the Ministry of Health is already preparing legislative changes in this area. The regulatory work programme of the Government of the Republic of Slovenia for 2016 provides for the amendment of the Health and Health Insurance Act, which provides for the regulation of the problem in question this year. For we believe that the employer must do everything in his power to ensure safety and health at work in accordance with the legislation in force in the field of protection at work and unconditionally do everything in his power. If, in terms of safety at work, the employer has all the measures, such as a safety statement with a risk assessment, pre-health checks of the staff, if the workers have a safety test at work, if the employer teaches them from the outside, gives guidance on measures to ensure protection at work and so on, it should also be borne in mind that an accident may have occurred by accident or is the responsibility of the worker himself. Although these cases of serious negligence are relatively small, employers point out that, in the case of regressive claims, the cost recovery may be so high that it can jeopardise the company’s survival, which is particularly true for smaller economic operators. Despite the fact that the scope of compensation claims in the case of employers' liability deserves to be found under existing legislation, we do not think that the solution we are considering today is the most appropriate one and that is why we will not support it in the Group of the Modern Centre. The proposal is contrary to the basic principle of compensation law, which states that the cause must be compensated in the amount caused or in the way of establishing the previous situation. The proposal also threatens the protection of a weaker customer in relation to the employer, a worker where the employer already has a number of obligations under the applicable legislation to ensure safe and healthy work. Finally, it should be borne in mind that the adoption of such a solution, contrary to the applicant's assessment, would have significant effects on the public funds collected by the Slovenian Health Insurance Office on the basis of the contributions collected, and we hope that in most cases it is justified. The decommissioning of these funds from the health register would mean less money for health care, which would indirectly harm all citizens. However, in cases that indicate that this is happening unjustifiablely, it is necessary to find a solution in due course that will be right for everyone, both for workers and for employers and all users of cash health care. Thank you.

In short, if an employer who has to have regulated certain papers in accordance with the legislation has to have a safety statement with a risk assessment, all his employees must have periodic health checks, must have a safety course at work, must have a consistent education in other matters, the employer must, at the time of continuous work, give continuous instructions to his employees on safety at work, must warn of any infringements of those rules, of course before any accident occurs at work, and, of course, must carry out the necessary supervision in accordance with this. If the employer has done all of this and there is a case of injury at work, of course, according to the legislation he must complete the form so-called IR8 form Accident report, injury at work which must be sent in four copies, as we know, to the personal physician of his employee, and then returned one copy to the employer and two copies, of course, to the Institute of Health Insurance of Slovenia. When the Institute of Health Insurance of Slovenia receives this report of injury at work and here is already the first potential problem, because all employers and those first legislative bases fail to meet, they say all the papers of education checks and so on, and even in the case of injury at work not all are as aware as they should be, and of course do not report this injury at work. The Health Insurance Office, when it receives this application, assesses whether it was the fault excluded from the employee or whether the fault excluded from the employer or whether it is possible to share the fault, we know that too. And, of course, those employers who have all the necessary legal basis for this area of safety at work and who report an injury in accordance with the legislation will not receive a regressive claim. Once again, those employers who work in accordance with the legislation will not receive a regression request. Tell me one who received a regression claim and all of these marriage grounds were met. Moreover, in only 1% of cases of injury at work, in only one case of all injuries occurring at work, there are those for which the ZZS sends a regression claim to the employer only 1%. Believe that in this 1%, it is not the exemplary employers who respect the law, but the ones who take it, let us put it this way, perhaps a little bit more with their left hand. The proposal of the proposer is very dangerous, where negligence starts to divide into large, small, medium and so on. Malpractice is negligence. Given that the employer is legally responsible in the workplace, then there can be little negligence. Either it is or it isn't. It is known what the employer must do, what he must ensure, what he must monitor, what he must warn about, and what he must do in case of injury or accident at work. If he does so, no employer from the ZZZ will receive a regression claim. In this case, those honest employers are already protected today and this amendment to the legislation is not necessary. I believe that the Ministry of Health Insurance in the coming Health Insurance Act will also review this area again and, if there is to be anything else to settle here, it will also settle, and until then, as it is said, I will not support this proposal. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2016-03-29-SDZ7-Redna-17.ana.u188	Well-known, distinguished Vice-President, distinguished Members of Parliament, distinguished Members of Parliament! With the proposed Resolution on the National Health Plan 2016-2025 called "Common for the Society of Health", which is ahead of you today, we will receive a strategic document in the Republic of Slovenia in the field of health. It sets out the objectives and aims of how we will respond to the changed needs of the population in the coming years, adjust the health care and insurance system and, above all, achieve the goal that the people of Slovenia will be healthier and have more healthy old years. In the national plan, we commit ourselves to the European values of health systems, such as universality, solidarity, justice, accessibility, security and quality. In the resolution, as we have said many times before, we have also entered the results that we have obtained in the analysis of the Slovenian healthcare system. We have also improved the proposal with a number of proposals from the public debate and several amendments. The resolution was also supported by the Socio-Economic Council, the competent commission of the Council of State, the Council of State and, unanimously, the Health Committee of the National Assembly. The document thus has specific objectives, activities and measures to achieve them and indicators with which we will regularly monitor progress. It is very important that the document commits the entire government to regard health as a value and a goal in all its policies. The resolution puts the user and the operator at the centre and seeks to strengthen and protect health and disease prevention, optimise healthcare, increase health care performance, fair, solidarity and sustainable financing of healthcare. Its adoption is also a condition for drawing on European resources under the operational programme for the implementation of cohesion policy in the 2014-2020 programme. And let me also convey the call of the European Commissioner, who recently visited Slovenia, who also visited Parliament and recognized the resolution as one of the best quality documents in the EU Member States and also recommended that you accept it. And what tasks does the resolution provide for in 2016? A key is the new Health and Health Insurance Act, which we will put to the debate in the first half of this year. The next challenging task for two ministries, the Ministry of Labour and our Ministry, is to prepare a law in long-term care. There are also two important laws in public debate, namely the Systemic Act on Pharmacy, which, for the first time since 1992, regulates pharmaceutical activities comprehensively for the first time since 1992. In the public debate, there is also a law restricting the consumption of tobacco and related products. In addition to legal changes, of course, we are planning a number of activities that do not necessarily require legislative changes, such as the establishment of a system of evaluation of health technologies, where we are working together in the European Union's project. An interdisciplinary working group preparing quality and safety indicators is already in place to establish a comprehensive system of quality and security. We continue with the pilot project of waiting periods, which will shorten waiting periods in a short time period and amend the Patient Rights Act and Rules in a long time. There is also a rules on occupational diseases in the final phase. We are preparing a strategy for the development of primary health care, which will include gynaecology, paediatrics, dentistry, health care and patronage. We will also start the process of renovating accounting models at all levels next year. Finally, let me thank all of us who have contributed with your arguments to the resolution and have enabled this strategic document in the field of health to come to life. Thank you.

The fact that commercial insurance companies make profits at the expense of the supplementary health insurance premium, which is essentially compulsory, is that, for various reasons, around 40,000 people are temporarily or permanently without compulsory health insurance, that about 100,000 people do not have supplementary health insurance, which means that no compulsory health insurance is available to them without high fees. If we add to this the fact that around 170,000 people with the lowest incomes mean a premium for supplementary health insurance disproportionately high costs, the change in the supplementary health insurance system is inevitable. We are committed to financing health care according to the wealth of individuals in society and to distributing health services according to the health needs of people, while ensuring that all citizens have the opportunity to meet their needs for quality and timely health care independently of their material situation. In drafting legislation, it will therefore be crucial for us Social Democrats that the content clearly sets out the objectives of law and law, among which the maintenance of public health must take the first place. We are insisting that there is a clear gap between public and private health activities. We support the regulation of the area of award of concessions in a way that we will award them on the basis of fixed-term tenders only where public health institutions are unable to provide the intended scope of services. We believe that the measures envisaged in the resolution follow the principles of maintaining public health and maintaining universal accessibility for all, and we will therefore support the motion for a resolution. ParlaMint-SI_2016-03-29-SDZ7-Redna-17.ana.u196	Good afternoon, thank you very much, Madam President. The basis and purpose of the resolution are the principle of solidarity and universality. The principle of solidarity is another name for the socialist maxim that everyone must contribute according to their capabilities and get the health services they need. However, the principle of universality means that we must be included in this system of solidarity for all. In the United Left, we consider that the resolution is a good basis for implementing these principles in all three areas covered by it — public health, health and health insurance. In all these areas, it would certainly be possible to set some goals even more courageously. The writers of this resolution are well aware that social inequality has a significant impact on health inequalities. Chronic diseases, risk factors and early death are significantly higher in people with low incomes, precarious forms of employment, and so on, more frequently in developing regions. In addition to the health programmes provided for in this resolution, the Government should immediately implement an ambitious programme to combat poverty, in particular between the elderly, single parent families and other vulnerable groups. The aim of the Ministry of Health is that in fact all permanent residents will be included in the compulsory insurance system. As regards health activities, we are pleased that this is the first official document to define the concession as a supplement, i.e. as an exception. This is a good basis for the development of health homes, which has been halted by the interests of concessionaires and the wrong policies of municipalities and health ministers. Already nearly 14% of public funds are spent by private concessionaires, while at the same time the quality and accessibility of healthcare is not improving. Nor has the work of doctors from public institutions in the private sector been systematically limited. While we would limit private practice more than this resolution, this resolution is certainly a sufficient basis for decisive action. Health care in Slovenia will not be universal if we do not extend compulsory health insurance rights through new treatment methods. If we are lagging behind, modern methods will become the privilege of rich and private performers. It is therefore necessary to increase the proportion of public funds that we as a society provide to healthcare. Slovenia’s problem is not so much that we spend 2 000 88 dollars per capita on health and in Austria, $5,000 427 dollars, but that we have only 6.1% of GDP public funds and 7.9, which is 1.8% more. There is a need for a conversion in fiscal policy. We urgently need to increase the share of the budget's revenue by increasing capital and property taxation. I would like to point out that by 2019 the government is planning a substantial reduction in GDP revenues, while at the same time predicting an increase in the share of military defence expenditure, police and some other sectors. In this way it is not possible to achieve the objectives of this declaration. Other essential sources are the replacement of supplementary health insurance with a solidarity public contribution. As long as citizens are forced to enter into commercial insurance for compulsory insurance rights, these services are not universally available to residents who are legally insured, but are not primarily financed by the principle of solidarity, and we have therefore proposed an improvement of the text with an amendment which has determined that people will not have to pay for services from baskets of rights, neither from pockets nor from insurance premiums. Given that this is enshrined in the Coalition Treaty, we are surprised that the coalition Members did not support this May. We increasingly doubt that they will also support the efforts of their own Minister of Health in this area. However, the fact is that if the government does not abolish supplementary insurance in this mandate, it will not only violate its coalition contract, but also the principles of fairness and solidarity enshrined in this resolution. Thank you very much.

The Law on the Ombudsman was adopted in 1993 and Article 1 provides for the establishment of the Ombudsman for the protection of human rights and fundamental freedoms in relation to State authorities, local self-government authorities and public authorities. Since then, the Ombudsman has been pressing the road and establishing a certain attitude towards all three branches of government in our country, so its independent and impartial action in protecting the rights of all citizens and citizens is all the more important. We are aware of the importance of appropriate and effective mechanisms for the protection of the rights of individuals in the modern centre Party Group and therefore we also consider the Institute of the Ombudsman to be an important democratic acquisition. Let us add that the Institute of the Ombudsman is a constitutional category set out in Article 159 of the Constitution of Slovenia. The Ombudsman's function is also an extremely responsible function, and it is also appropriate to appoint the guardian's deputies on a case-by-case basis. The Law on the Ombudsman provides that the Ombudsman shall be independent and independent in his work and shall comply with the provisions of the Constitution and international human rights and fundamental freedoms legal acts. Article 15 of the Act also provides that the Ombudsman shall have at least two and a maximum of four deputies appointed by the Ombudsman on a proposal from the National Assembly. So today, on the agenda, we have a proposal to appoint a Deputy Ombudsman, who has received sufficient support already on the Commission's terms of reference. In the SMC Group, when choosing a candidate, we trust the Ombudsman's judgement and believe that the proposed candidate has the appropriate qualifications to perform such an important and responsible function. The areas of work that the candidate will perform require a great deal of work, professional reference, broad visibility, an excellent knowledge of the legal and constitutional order of our country and of the international institutions. When we discuss respect for human rights, freedoms and fundamental human dignity, the expertise, the high reference competence and, above all, the autonomy and independence of candidates representing the institution of the Ombudsman are essential. We will support the proposed candidate in the SMC Group of Members. ParlaMint-SI_2016-03-29-SDZ7-Redna-17.ana.u224	Ms Vesna Vervega has an explanation of the vote on behalf of the modern centre Party's Group.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-03-29-SDZ7-Redna-17.ana.u225	Thank you for your word. Your Honor! The new Environmental Protection Act, which we are deciding about again, does not reduce the quality standards of the environment. The regulation of environmental standards in law itself is difficult enough and also contains sufficient safety mechanisms. The 10th paragraph of Article 10 of the amendment provides that the Ministry may set less stringent emission limit values than determined on the basis of the BAT conclusions, and that this limit value may not exceed the limit values prescribed under Article 17 of this Law, which states that the level of environmental protection achieved may not deteriorate and that the emission limit values and other requirements may also be set more stringently than those laid down in the BAT conclusions. The 12th paragraph of this Article also states that the less stringent emission limit values referred to in this paragraph cannot be established if this would jeopardise the achievement of the environmental quality standards referred to in Article 23 of this Law, or if the device is located in the area referred to in Article 24 of this Law, thus to the degraded area. We have also heard that the Ministry of the Environment is planning an additional arrangement, by amending the IED Regulation, by also setting out more detailed guidelines for the preparation of an assessment of disproportionately higher costs compared to environmental benefits. Likewise, in the SMC Group, we do not agree that the termination of the environmental authorisation period means, in fact, the exclusion of the public from the issuing or modifying of the environmental permit, since no article of the law governing public participation procedures changes. That's why we're going to support the new law.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-03-29-SDZ7-Redna-17.ana.u226	Mrs Marjana Kotnik Popot has an explanation of vote on behalf of the Desus Group.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-03-29-SDZ7-Redna-17.ana.u227	Thank you for your word. When we re-decision on the Law on amending and supplementing the Law on the Protection of the Environment, we would first talk about the issues which sparked the most opposition during the debate. Before that, I would say that the amendment does not lower environmental standards. With regard to the elimination of the time limit on the validity of the environmental licence, it should be said that Directive 2010/75 does not contain a requirement for Member States to limit the validity of authorisations. The Republic of Slovenia has been among the rare countries, if not the only ones where such a time limit has been imposed. The Directive only requires the competent authority to verify at regular intervals and, if necessary, update the conditions in the authorisation. The applicable law in Article 77 lays down the obligations of the competent authority to review and amend the applicable authorisation for a particular device whenever its operator intends to make an amendment to the device. The applicable law also lays down, together with the proposed amendments and additions in Article 78, the obligation of the competent authority to review and amend the applicable environmental authorisation in certain cases following an official duty. I would also like to point out that the proposed addition to the current Regulation, by which the competent authority is required to review and, if necessary, amend the environmental protection authorisation every 10 years if the environmental protection authorisation was not amended at that time for any other reason. The public and the public concerned also have the same rights after amending the current regime as regards participation in the authorisation and modification procedures as in the applicable regulation in accordance with the requirements of the relevant Directives. The public therefore has every right to include and co-decision in the environmental authorisation process. In view of the possibilities for certain less stringent limit values, the Ministry will be able to set less stringent emission limit values than determined on the basis of BAT conclusions, only if a specific condition or conditions are met. Once again, I stress that regulation is difficult enough and that is why the environment will not be destroyed in Slovenia. In the Desus Group, we will therefore back up the amendment of the Environmental Protection Act. Thank you very much.
